=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
Revision History
V2.5.0
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2026-09-29
Change Description
1. Version updated to V2.5.0
V2.4.0
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2026-01-15
Change Description
1. Added GPU operator support documentation
2. Added Pow/CumSum to the CPU operator support list
3. Added ReduceMax+exConvStreaming operator documentation for the 3588 NPU
implementation
4. Added ReduceMax/exConvStreaming/Sin/Cos operator documentation for the 3576 NPU
implementation
5. Added ReduceMax+exConvStreaming operator documentation for the 1126B NPU
implementation
V2.3.2
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2025-03-28
Change Description
1. Added RV1126B platform documentation
2. Added RV1106B platform documentation
3. Updated the Conv Dilation parameter restrictions for all platforms
4. Updated the Resize operator restrictions
V2.3.0
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2024-11-04
Change Description
1. Added documentation for the mean_var mode of exNorm on the
RK3576/RK3588/RK3562/RK3568 platforms
- 21 -

=== PDF PAGE 22 ===
2. MAX/MIN OPs on all platforms now support Broadcast
3. Modiﬁed the RK3576 Softmax restriction speciﬁcations
V2.2.0
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2024-09-03
Change Description
1. Modiﬁed the softmax speciﬁcation restrictions on all platforms (except rk3568), and added
softmax splitting
2. Added RMSNorm support for RK3576, RK3588, RK3562, and RK3568
V2.1.0
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2024-08-07
Change Description
1. Added RK2118 documentation
2. Added RV1103B documentation
3. Updated operator speciﬁcations for all platforms
4. Added RK3576 operator speciﬁcations (including Hardmax, ConvLut, etc.)
5. RK2118 exConvStreaming support
6. Added FlashAttentionV2 optimization support to the RK3562/RK3576 exSDPA operator
V2.0.0-beta
Author: NPU Team
Date: 2024-03-22
Change Description
1. Added RK3576 platform operator support speciﬁcations
2. Added hardware support speciﬁcations for exSDPAttention and exMatMul on the
RK3588/RK3562/RK3576 platforms
3. Added Floor, Mod, And, GatherElements, and exMeanVarianceNormalization CPU operator
support
4. Added RK2118 platform operator support speciﬁcations
- 22 -

=== PDF PAGE 23 ===
NPU OP Speciﬁcations
RK3566/RK3568
Add
Performs element-wise binary addition(C=A+B),supporting multi-directional NumPy-style
broadcasting
Inputs
A:T1,T2
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Broadcasting support
Supports broadcasting along arbitrary dimensions
Broadcasting constraints are the same as the Expand operator
B:T1,T2
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Broadcasting support
Supports broadcasting along arbitrary dimensions
Broadcasting constraints are the same as the Expand operator
Outputs
C:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
T2:ﬂoat(constTensor)
AddRelu
- 23 -

=== PDF PAGE 24 ===
Add and Relu fused computation, C=Relu(A+B)
Inputs
Same as Add
Outputs
Same as Add
Other Support
Multi-core: Supported
BatchNormalization
Performs batch normalization on the input tensor according to the description in
https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.03167
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
epsilon: ﬂoat (default value = 1e-5)
A real number added when dividing by the standard deviation to prevent division by 0
epsilon∈(0,∞]
momentum:ﬂoat
Sliding average parameters during training
- 24 -

=== PDF PAGE 25 ===
Concat
Concatenate a sequence of vectors into a single vector along the axis speciﬁed by axis. All
vectors must be the same size except in the axis dimension
Inputs
input_tensor:T
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Outputs
output:T
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Data Type Constraints
T:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
axisint64
axis∈{0,1,2,3}
Convolution
Convolution
Inputs
input_tensor:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
width: when dilated_kernel_h>1, width<16383. In addition, the ﬁrst-layer input
width is restricted; see the model input description for details.
Quantization support
per-layer
weight:T1
shape:[output_channel,input_channel,kernel_h,kernel_w]
kernel_h∈[1,31]
- 25 -

=== PDF PAGE 26 ===
kernel_w∈[1,31]
Quantization support
per-layer
per-channel
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Attributes
strides:int(strides_h,strides_w)
stride_h∈[1,7]
stride_w∈[1,7]
pads:int(pads_top,pads_left,pads_bottom,pads_right)
pads_top∈[0,15]
pads_left∈[0,15]
pads_bottom∈[0,15]
pads_right∈[0,15]
group:int
dilations:int(dilations_h,dilations_w)
dilations_h∈[1,31]
dilations_w∈[1,31]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Depthwise Convolution
Depthwise separable convolution
Inputs
input_tensor:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
width: when dilated_kernel_h>1, width<16383. In addition, the ﬁrst-layer input
width is restricted; see the model input description for details.

=== PDF PAGE 27 ===
Quantization support
per-layer
weight:T1
shape:[output_channel,input_channel,kernel_h,kernel_w]
kernel_h∈[1,8]
kernel_w∈[1,8]
Quantization support
per-layer
per-channel
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Attributes
strides:int(strides_h,strides_w)
stride_h∈[1,7]
stride_w∈[1,7]
pads:int(pads_top,pads_left,pads_bottom,pads_right)
pads_top∈[0,15]
pads_left∈[0,15]
pads_bottom∈[0,15]
pads_right∈[0,15]
group:int

dilations_h∈[1,32]
dilations_w∈[1,32]
Data Type Constraints

ConvolutionRelu
- 27 -

=== PDF PAGE 28 ===
Convolution and Relu fused computation, Output=Relu(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionClip
Convolution and Clip fused computation, Output=Clip(Conv(Input)). Description and speciﬁcation
constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionPRelu/LeakyRelu
Convolution and PRelu/LeakyRelu fused computation, Output = PRelu(Conv(Input)) or Output =
LeakyRelu(Conv(Input)). Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionAdd
Convolution and Add fused computation, Output=Add(Conv(Input0),Input1). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionSigmoid
Convolution and Sigmoid fused computation, Output=Sigmoid(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionTanh
Convolution and Tanh fused computation, Output=Tanh(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionSoftplus
Convolution and Softplus fused computation, Output=Softplus(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionHardSigmoid
Convolution and HardSigmoid fused computation, Output=HardSigmoid(Conv(Input)).
Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionHardSwish
- 28 -

=== PDF PAGE 29 ===
Convolution and HardSwish fused computation, Output=HardSwish(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionElu
Convolution and Elu fused computation, Output=Elu(Conv(Input)). Description and speciﬁcation
constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionSwish
Convolution and Swish fused computation, Output=Swish(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionMish
Convolution and Mish fused computation, Output=Mish(Conv(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvolutionAddRelu
Convolution and Add and Relu fused computation, Output=Relu(Add(Conv(Input0),Input1)).
Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as Convolution
ConvTranspose
Transposed convolution
Inputs
input_tensor:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
width: when dilated_kernel_h>1, width<16383. In addition, the ﬁrst-layer input
width is restricted; see the model input description for details.
Quantization support
per-layer
weight:T1
shape:[output_channel,input_channel,kernel_h,kernel_w]
kernel_h∈[1,31]
kernel_w∈[1,31]
Quantization support
- 29 -

=== PDF PAGE 30 ===
per-layer
per-channel
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Quantization support
per-layer
Attributes
strides:int(strides_h,strides_w)
stride_h only supports 1,2,4,8
stride_w only supports 1,2,4,8
pads:int(pads_top,pads_left,pads_bottom,pads_right) Note when setting pad:
kernel_hdilations_h-dilations_h-pads_top<0 is not supported; kernel_wdilations_w-
dilations_w-pads_left<0 is not supported; stride_h(height-1)-pads_top+1<output_h is not
supported; stride_w(width-1)-pads_left+1<output_w is not supported
pads_top∈[0,15]
pads_left∈[0,15]
pads_bottom∈[0,15]
pads_right∈[0,15]
group:int
group: only 1 is supported; num_output is supported if and only if
num_input=num_output
dilations:int(dilations_h,dilations_w)
dilations_h∈[1,32]
dilations_w∈[1,32]
When dilation is not equal to (1,1), only kernel=(1,1) is supported
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
ConvTranposeRelu
ConvTranspose and Relu fused computation, Output=Relu(ConvTranspose(Input)). Description
and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose
- 30 -

=== PDF PAGE 31 ===
ConvTranposeClip
ConvTranspose and Clip fused computation, Output=Clip(ConvTranspose(Input)). Description and
speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose
ConvTranposePRelu/LeakyRelu
ConvTranspose and PRelu/LeakyRelu fused computation, Output=PRelu(ConvTranspose(Input)) or
Output= LeakyRelu(ConvTranspose(Input)). Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the
same as ConvTranspose
ConvTranposeAdd
ConvTranspose and Add fused computation, Output=Add(ConvTranspose(Input0),Input1).
Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose
ConvTranposeSigmoid
ConvTranspose and Sigmoid fused computation, Output=Sigmoid(ConvTranspose(Input)).
Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose
ConvTranposeTanh
ConvTranspose and Tanh fused computation, Output=Tanh(ConvTranspose(Input)). Description
and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose
ConvTranposeSoftplus
ConvTranspose and Softplus fused computation, Output=Softplus(ConvTranspose(Input)).
Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose
ConvTranposeHardSigmoid
ConvTranspose and HardSigmoid fused computation,
Output=HardSigmoid(ConvTranspose(Input))
ConvTranposeHardSwish
ConvTranspose and HardSwish fused computation, Output=HardSwish(ConvTranspose(Input)).
Description and speciﬁcation constraints are the same as ConvTranspose

GRU
Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU). GRU extensions and variants are named exGRU operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exGRU. The computation formulas
are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh): r_t = f(x_t · W_r + h_{t−1} · R_r + Wb_r +
Rb_r) z_t = f(x_t · W_z + h_{t−1} · R_z + Wb_z + Rb_z) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + (r_t ⊙ h_{t−1}) · R_h
+ Rb_h + Wb_h), (WRB=0) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + r_t ⊙ (h_{t−1} · R_h + Rb_h)) + Wb_h, (WRB=1)
H_t = (1 − z_t) ⊙ h_t + z_t ⊙ h_{t−1}
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[seq_length,batch_size,input_size]
seqlength∈(0,8192]
batchsize∈(0,8192], when greater than 1, only multiples of 4 are supported
inputsize∈(0,8192]
Quantization support
per-layer
W:T1
shape:[num_directions,4*hidden_size,input_size]
inputsize∈(0,8192]
hiddensize∈(0,8192]
numdirections:1or2
Quantization support
per-layer
per-channel
R:T1
shape:[num_directions,4*hidden_size,hidden_size]
hiddensize∈(0,8192]
numdirections:1or2
Quantization support
per-layer
per-channel

hiddensize∈(0,8192]

LSTM
Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM). LSTM extensions and variants are named exLSTM operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exLSTM. The computation
formulas are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh,h=Tanh): i_t = f(x_t · W_i +
h_{t−1} · R_i + Wb_i + Rb_i) f_t = f(x_t · W_f + h_{t−1} · R_f + Wb_f + Rb_f) c_t = f(x_t · W_c +
h_{t−1} · R_c + Wb_c + Rb_c) o_t = f(x_t · W_o + h_{t−1} · R_o + Wb_o + Rb_o) C_t = f_t ⊙
C_{t−1} + i_t ⊙ c_t h_t = o_t ⊙ h(C_t)

MaxPool
- 44 -

=== PDF PAGE 45 ===
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]
height∈(0,8192]
width∈(0,8192]
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]

kernel_h∈(0,7]
kernel_w∈(0,7]
auto_pad:string
Padding mode: only NOTSET is supported
ceil_mode:int64
Whether to use ceil or ﬂoor to compute the output shape: not supported
strides:int64(strides_h,strides_w)
strides_h∈(0,8]
strides_w∈(0,8]
count_include_pad:int64[]
count_include_pad, whether to include pad values in computation: 1
pad:int64(pad_top,pad_left,pad_bottom,pad_right)
pad_top∈[0,7]
pad_left∈[0,7]
pad_bottom∈[0,7]
pad_right∈[0,7]
storage_order:int64
The preferred storage mode: 0

exWindow
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 70 -

=== PDF PAGE 71 ===
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.

dilations_w-pads_left<0 is not supported; stride_h(height-1)-pads_top+1<output_h is not
supported; stride_w(width-1)-pads_left+1<output_w is not supported
pads_top∈[0,15]
pads_left∈[0,15]
pads_bottom∈[0,15]
pads_right∈[0,15]
group:int
group: only 1 is supported; num_output is supported if and only if
num_input=num_output
dilations:int(dilations_h,dilations_w)
dilations_h∈[1,32]
dilations_w∈[1,32]
When dilation is not equal to (1,1), only kernel=(1,1) is supported
Data Type Constraints

GRU
Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU). GRU extensions and variants are named exGRU operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exGRU. The computation formulas
- 83 -

=== PDF PAGE 84 ===
are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh): r_t = f(x_t · W_r + h_{t−1} · R_r + Wb_r +
Rb_r) z_t = f(x_t · W_z + h_{t−1} · R_z + Wb_z + Rb_z) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + (r_t ⊙ h_{t−1}) · R_h
+ Rb_h + Wb_h), (WRB=0) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + r_t ⊙ (h_{t−1} · R_h + Rb_h)) + Wb_h, (WRB=1)
H_t = (1 − z_t) ⊙ h_t + z_t ⊙ h_{t−1}

LSTM
Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM). LSTM extensions and variants are named exLSTM operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exLSTM. The computation
formulas are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh,h=Tanh): i_t = f(x_t · W_i +
h_{t−1} · R_i + Wb_i + Rb_i) f_t = f(x_t · W_f + h_{t−1} · R_f + Wb_f + Rb_f) c_t = f(x_t · W_c +
h_{t−1} · R_c + Wb_c + Rb_c) o_t = f(x_t · W_o + h_{t−1} · R_o + Wb_o + Rb_o) C_t = f_t ⊙
C_{t−1} + i_t ⊙ c_t h_t = o_t ⊙ h(C_t)

MaxPool
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]

Softmax
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
Softmax(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.

RM SNorm(x)= x

exWindow
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
Inputs
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 127 -

=== PDF PAGE 128 ===
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.

GRU
Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU). GRU extensions and variants are named exGRU operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exGRU. The computation formulas
are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh): r_t = f(x_t · W_r + h_{t−1} · R_r + Wb_r +
Rb_r) z_t = f(x_t · W_z + h_{t−1} · R_z + Wb_z + Rb_z) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + (r_t ⊙ h_{t−1}) · R_h
+ Rb_h + Wb_h), (WRB=0) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + r_t ⊙ (h_{t−1} · R_h + Rb_h)) + Wb_h, (WRB=1)
H_t = (1 − z_t) ⊙ h_t + z_t ⊙ h_{t−1}
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[seq_length,batch_size,input_size]
seqlength∈(0,8192]
batchsize∈(0,8192], when greater than 1, only multiples of 4 are supported
inputsize∈(0,8192]
Quantization support
per-layer

C_{t−1} + i_t ⊙ c_t h_t = o_t ⊙ h(C_t)

MaxPool
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]
height∈(0,8192]
width∈(0,8192]
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]

kernel_h∈(0,7]
kernel_w∈(0,7]

Softmax
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
Softmax(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Subject to the transpose speciﬁcation constraints
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]

exWindow
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
- 178 -

=== PDF PAGE 179 ===
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 179 -

=== PDF PAGE 180 ===
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.

MaxPool
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]
height∈(0,8192]
width∈(0,8192]
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Attributes

kernel_h∈(0,7]
kernel_w∈(0,7]
auto_pad:string
Padding mode: only NOTSET is supported

Softmax
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
Softmax(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Subject to the transpose speciﬁcation constraints
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)

perm=[3,2,1,0]

exWindow
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
- 225 -

=== PDF PAGE 226 ===
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 226 -

=== PDF PAGE 227 ===
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.

broadcasting rule is similar to numpy.array(input) * numpy.ones(shape): dimensions are right-

GRU
Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU). GRU extensions and variants are named exGRU operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exGRU. The computation formulas
are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh): r_t = f(x_t · W_r + h_{t−1} · R_r + Wb_r +
Rb_r) z_t = f(x_t · W_z + h_{t−1} · R_z + Wb_z + Rb_z) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + (r_t ⊙ h_{t−1}) · R_h
+ Rb_h + Wb_h), (WRB=0) h_t = g(x_t · W_h + r_t ⊙ (h_{t−1} · R_h + Rb_h)) + Wb_h, (WRB=1)
H_t = (1 − z_t) ⊙ h_t + z_t ⊙ h_{t−1}
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[seq_length,batch_size,input_size]
seqlength∈(0,8192]
batchsize∈(0,8192], when greater than 1, only multiples of 4 are supported
inputsize∈(0,8192]
Quantization support
per-layer
- 239 -

=== PDF PAGE 240 ===
W:T1
shape:[num_directions,4*hidden_size,input_size]
inputsize∈(0,8192]
hiddensize∈(0,8192]
numdirections:1or2
Quantization support
per-layer
per-channel
R:T1
shape:[num_directions,4*hidden_size,hidden_size]
hiddensize∈(0,8192]

formulas are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh,h=Tanh): i_t = f(x_t · W_i +
h_{t−1} · R_i + Wb_i + Rb_i) f_t = f(x_t · W_f + h_{t−1} · R_f + Wb_f + Rb_f) c_t = f(x_t · W_c +
h_{t−1} · R_c + Wb_c + Rb_c) o_t = f(x_t · W_o + h_{t−1} · R_o + Wb_o + Rb_o) C_t = f_t ⊙
C_{t−1} + i_t ⊙ c_t h_t = o_t ⊙ h(C_t)

MaxPool
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]

When height*width==1&&channel%subc==0&&starts[1]%subc==0&&ends[1]% when

run on the NPU when steps==[1,1,1,1]
Softmax
- 263 -

=== PDF PAGE 264 ===
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
Softmax(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.

exConvStreaming
To reduce the latency of speech models, the input of the original Convolution is divided into

exWindow
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
- 284 -

=== PDF PAGE 285 ===
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor),ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 285 -

=== PDF PAGE 286 ===
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.
Other Support
Multi-core: Not yet supported

LSTM
Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM). LSTM extensions and variants are named exLSTM operators.
Parameter items marked (extern) are parameters exclusive to exLSTM. The computation
formulas are as follows: Equations(Default:f=Sigmoid,g=Tanh,h=Tanh): i_t = f(x_t · W_i +
h_{t−1} · R_i + Wb_i + Rb_i) f_t = f(x_t · W_f + h_{t−1} · R_f + Wb_f + Rb_f) c_t = f(x_t · W_c +
h_{t−1} · R_c + Wb_c + Rb_c) o_t = f(x_t · W_o + h_{t−1} · R_o + Wb_o + Rb_o) C_t = f_t ⊙
C_{t−1} + i_t ⊙ c_t h_t = o_t ⊙ h(C_t)

shape:[seq_length,batch_size,input_size]

MaxPool
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]
height∈(0,8192]
width∈(0,8192]
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Attributes
kernel_shape:int64(kernel_h,kernel_w)
kernel_h∈(0,7]
kernel_w∈(0,7]
Note: kernel_h=1 and kernel_w=1 are not supported
auto_pad:string
Padding mode: only NOTSET is supported
ceil_mode:int64
Whether to use ceil or ﬂoor to compute the output shape: not supported
strides:int64(strides_h,strides_w)
strides_h∈(0,8]
strides_w∈(0,8]
count_include_pad:int64[]
count_include_pad, whether to include pad values in computation: 1
pad:int64(pad_top,pad_left,pad_bottom,pad_right)
pad_top∈[0,7]
pad_left∈[0,7]
pad_bottom∈[0,7]
- 302 -

=== PDF PAGE 303 ===
pad_right∈[0,7]
Data Type Constraints

Softmax
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
Softmax(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]
width: · axis=1,No limitation; · axis=3/-1,width∈(0,8192] and subject to the
transpose speciﬁcation constraints
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
- 310 -

=== PDF PAGE 311 ===
axis:int64
The axis for softmax: 1,3, i.e., channel and width directions
exSoftmax13
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
exSoftmax13(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.
Inputs

exConvStreaming
To reduce the latency of speech models, the input of the original Convolution is divided into
several time-frame slices. Each time-frame slice is used as input to compute the convolution, and
the results are cached for subsequent time frames, thereby achieving streaming audio
processing.

exWindow
- 324 -

=== PDF PAGE 325 ===
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:ﬂoat16(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 325 -

=== PDF PAGE 326 ===
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.

Multi-core: Not yet supported

Broadcast the input tensor according to the given shape and broadcasting rules. The
broadcasting rule is similar to numpy.array(input) * numpy.ones(shape): dimensions are right-
aligned; two corresponding dimensions must have the same value, or one of them equals 1

MaxPool
MaxPool consumes an input tensor X and applies max pooling to the tensor according to kernel
size, stride size, and padding length. Max pooling consists of computing the maximum over all
values of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel size, and downsampling the data
into output tensor Y for further processing. The output spatial shape calculation diﬀers
depending on whether explicit padding (when using pads) or automatic padding (when using
auto_pad) is used. Only explicit padding is used
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
channel∈(0,8192]
height∈(0,7]
width∈(0,7]
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Attributes
kernel_shape:int64(kernel_h,kernel_w)
kernel_h∈(0,7]
kernel_w∈(0,7]
auto_pad:string
Padding mode: only NOTSET is supported
ceil_mode:int64
Whether to use ceil or ﬂoor to compute the output shape: not supported
strides:int64(strides_h,strides_w)
strides_h∈(0,8]
strides_w∈(0,8]
count_include_pad:int64[]
- 339 -

pad_bottom∈[0,7]
pad_right∈[0,7]

Softmax
This operator computes the normalized exponential value of the given input:
Softmax(input, axis) = exp(input − max(input, axis)) / Σ_axis exp(input −
max(input, axis))
The "axis" attribute indicates the dimension along which Softmax is performed. The output
tensor has the same shape and contains the Softmax values of the corresponding input.
Inputs
input:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Subject to the transpose speciﬁcation constraints
Outputs
output:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor)
Attributes
axis:int64
The axis for softmax: 1,3, i.e., channel and width directions

c_type: strings (default "add")
add and mul are optional, indicating whether C is added or multiplied
exWindow
exWindow originates from the window_partition and window_reverse operations of
swin_transformer. In the original paper, input features are divided into multiple non-overlapping
windows, each of size window_size × window_size. Both input and output features are required to
be 4-dimensional shapes, partitioned in both height and width directions. The pytorch
implementation is as follows:
- 360 -

=== PDF PAGE 361 ===
Inputs
X:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Outputs
Y:T1
shape:[batch,channel,height,width]
Data Type Constraints
T1:int8(Tensor)
Attributes
mode:string
Supported modes: partition, reverse, partition_num_ﬁrst
partition: implemented with reference to window_partition;
reverse: implemented with reference to window_reverse;
partition_num_ﬁrst: the diﬀerence from partition is that the H,W of
partition_num_ﬁrst are divided according to num_windows, while the H,W of
def window_partition(x, window_size):
    """
    Args:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
        window_size (int): window size
    Returns:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
    """
    B, C, H, W = x.shape
    x = x.view(B, C, H // window_size, window_size, W // window_size, window_size)
    windows = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, -1, window_size * 
window_size)
    return windows
def window_reverse(windows, window_size, H, W):
    """
    Args:
        windows: (B, C, num_windows * num_windows, window_size * window_size)
        window_size (int): Window size
        H (int): Height of image
        W (int): Width of image
    Returns:
        x: (B, C, H, W)
    """
    B, C, _, _ = windows.shape
    x = windows.view(B, C, H // window_size, W // window_size, window_size, 
window_size)
    x = x.permute(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).contiguous().view(B, C, H, W)
    return x
- 361 -

=== PDF PAGE 362 ===
partition are divided according to window_sizes.
window_sizes:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the window size. Deﬁnes the height and width of each window.
Attention computation and feature extraction within the window are based on this size.
num_windows:listoﬁnts
A 2-dimensional vector, the number of windows. Deﬁnes how many windows the height
and width of the input feature are divided into.
Other Support
Multi-core: Not yet supported

- 377 -

=== PDF PAGE 378 ===
Model I/O Limitations
Model Input Limitations
1. The alignment constraints apply only to the zero-copy API; the normal API has no such
constraints.
2. The input width alignment requirement can be queried from the w_stride attribute of the
zero-copy API. Note: w_stride cannot be changed.
3. Only the input width has alignment requirements under diﬀerent channel conditions; there
are no other constraints.
4. If mean and scale are not needed for the input, set mean to 0 and scale to 1.
5. If channel > 4, mean/scale uniformly use the ﬁrst value, i.e., mean[0] and scale[0].
6. If the ﬁrst layer is ﬂoating-point type, there is no quant operation.
7. RV1106/RV1103/RV1103B do not support CPU mean/scale/quant operations.
8. For detailed usage, refer to "Rockchip_RKNPU_User_Guide_RKNN_SDK".
RK3566/RK3568
RK3588

=== PDF PAGE 381 ===
RV1103B
Model Output Limitations
1. If the output tensor type is NHWC and the output conversion is implemented by the NPU,
there are alignment requirements; CPU implementations have no alignment requirements.
2. The output precision type int8/ﬂoat16 indicates the data type of the raw output of the last
layer of the model.
3. For NCHW output, if implemented by the NPU with the zero-copy interface, the output
memory size should be based on the queried size.
4. For NC1HWC2 output, the output memory size should be based on the queried size.
